─── package.json:6-8 ───
[maintainability · low] This is an ESM package (`"type": "module"`) with a CLI bin but no `exports`
field. Without `exports`, deep imports like `import 'github-tui/tui/foo.js'` are technically
unsupported by the package contract, and tooling can't tree-shake reliably. Add an `"exports"` map
(even a minimal `{".": "./app.mjs"}`) to formalize the public API.

  "bin": {
      "github-tui": "app.mjs"
+   },
+   "exports": {
+     ".": "./app.mjs"

─── tests/github-errors.test.mjs:43-46 ───
[maintainability · high] The `shellEscape` helper is copy-pasted from `tui/custom-keys.mjs` (per the
comment "mirrors custom-keys.mjs") rather than imported. If the production implementation is ever
tightened — e.g. to also reject newlines, escape `!` for history expansion, or handle non-string
inputs — these tests will continue to pass against the stale local copy and silently lose their
safety guarantee. Import the canonical implementation (or extract it to a shared utility module that
both files import) so this test file actually guards the live code.

```js
import { shellEscape } from '../tui/custom-keys.mjs';
```



─── tests/github-errors.test.mjs:57-57 ───
[test · medium] Misleading test description: "escapes semicolons" implies the function performs
special escaping on `;`, but it does not — it merely single-quote-wraps the whole string, which is
what neutralizes the semicolon in POSIX sh. Phrase the name (or its comment) in terms of the actual
mechanism so future readers don't believe there is bespoke metacharacter handling, and so a future
change that drops the single-quote wrapping is unambiguously caught by a regression in this test.

```js
// Wraps a payload containing ';' so POSIX sh treats it as a literal,
// not a command separator.
it('single-quote-wraps values containing shell metacharacters', () => {
  assert.equal(shellEscape('foo; rm -rf ~'), "'foo; rm -rf ~'");
});
```



─── tests/github-errors.test.mjs:64-64 ───
[test · medium] Same misleading-wording issue as the semicolon test: backticks aren't "escaped" by
`shellEscape`; they're rendered inert by the surrounding single quotes. Rename to describe the
actual mechanism so the test remains meaningful if the wrapping is ever weakened.

```js
it('single-quote-wraps values containing backticks', () => {
  assert.equal(shellEscape('foo`whoami`'), "'foo`whoami`'");
});
```



─── tests/github-errors.test.mjs:77-80 ───
[test · medium] `shellEscape` test coverage is thin for a security-relevant helper. Add cases for:
(a) newline injection (`'foo\nrm -rf ~'`), (b) `$(cmd)` and `${var}` command substitution, (c) a
value containing a literal backslash, and (d) a non-string input (number or object) to pin down
`String(value)` behavior. As written, a regression that drops the single-quote wrapping, removes the
apostrophe escape, or relies on `String()` for non-string values would not be detected.

```js
it('neutralizes newline injection', () => {
  assert.equal(shellEscape('foo\nrm -rf ~'), "'foo\nrm -rf ~'");
});
it('neutralizes $(...) command substitution', () => {
  assert.equal(shellEscape('$(whoami)'), "'$(whoami)'");
});
it('coerces non-string inputs through String()', () => {
  assert.equal(shellEscape(42), "'42'");
});
```

─── tests/github-errors.test.mjs:43-46 ───
[test · low] The falsy guard `if (!value)` treats `0`, `false`, and `NaN` identically to
`null`/`undefined`/`''` and emits `''`. There is no test pinning down the intended behavior for
these falsy-but-non-empty values; if `custom-keys.mjs` ever passes a numeric `0` (e.g. a count
placeholder) or a boolean `false`, it will silently become an empty shell argument. Add an explicit
assertion documenting the chosen semantics so the contract is part of the suite.

```js
it('treats numeric 0 as empty (document falsy semantics)', () => {

});
```



─── tests/check-imports.test.mjs:27-33 ───
[bug · high] `spawnSync` failures are not handled. If `process.execPath` is missing, the tool
crashes, or the 30s timeout elapses, `result.status` is `null`, `result.signal` is set (e.g.
`'SIGTERM'`), and `result.stderr` can be `null`. Every subsequent `assert.equal(result.status, 0/1,
...)`, `assert.match(result.stderr, ...)`, and `result.stderr.includes(...)` will then produce
confusing errors (`null === 0`) or throw `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading
'includes')` instead of a meaningful diagnostic. Inspect `result.error` / `result.signal` first and
rethrow with context, or fail fast before touching `stderr`/`stdout`.



─── tests/check-imports.test.mjs:37-41 ───
[maintainability · medium] The cleanup `try { unlinkSync(path); } catch {}` silently swallows every
error — including `EACCES`/`EPERM` from a permission issue, or `ENOTDIR` from a stale symlink. A
real cleanup failure now masquerades as success and the orphan file pollutes later runs without any
signal. At minimum log to `console.warn`, or assert the path is gone with `existsSync` after unlink.
Also, there is no `before`/`beforeEach` hook that prunes orphan `_check_imports_*.mjs` files from
`tui/`, so a previously-crashed run silently breaks the "clean codebase" test.



─── tests/check-imports.test.mjs:47-47 ───

`/^[^,\n]*0 issues\s*$/m` or check that the line is exactly `0 issues` / `0 issues found`. Otherwise
a regression in the tool's summary formatting could pass silently.

─── tests/check-imports.test.mjs:44-48 ───
[test · medium] "clean codebase" is the first declared test, but there is no `before`/`beforeEach`

─── tests/contributions.test.mjs:65-66 ───

[test · high] Control-byte filter coverage is too narrow. Only `\r`, `\n`, `\t`, and `\x7f` are
asserted as stripped from a paste body. A paste can embed bytes that have side effects inside
`handleInputKey` (or in the underlying readline): `\x03` (ETX / Ctrl-C), `\x04` (EOT / Ctrl-D, often
EOF), `\x1a` (SUB — caught by some terminals as SIGTSTP), `\x00` (NUL), and a bare `\x1b` (ESC)
inside the body. None of these are asserted. A regression that lets any of these leak through could
cause a paste to unexpectedly cancel the modal, submit, or terminate input. Add cases that assert
these bytes are stripped AND that `inputMode` stays `'input'` and `isPasting()` stays `false`
afterwards.

- it('long chunk with \\\\r, \\\\t, and DEL (0x7f) is filtered', () => {
-     // \x7f is the DEL byte (charCode 127) — terminals sometimes embed it.
-     handleInputKey('tok\r\t\x7fen\r\n');
+ it('long chunk with NUL / ETX / EOT / SUB / ESC embedded is filtered', () => {
+     // \x00 NUL, \x03 ETX (Ctrl-C), \x04 EOT (Ctrl-D), \x1a SUB, \x1b ESC.
+     handleInputKey('tok\x00\x03\x04\x1a\x1ben\r\n');
      assert.equal(appState.inputBuffer, 'token');
      assert.equal(appState.inputMode, 'input');
+     assert.equal(isPasting(), false);
    });

[bug · high] Source-level grep assertions do not verify behavior — they verify the source *contains*

(different quote style, added whitespace, helper extraction, rename of an identifier) silently

to `appState.token`, `appState.notifications`, `appState.inboxFilter`, etc. Each saves/restores in a
`finally` block via `Object.assign(appState, saved)`, but this pattern is only safe under sequential
execution. If Node's test runner is configured with `concurrency: true`, an interleaved
`Object.assign` in one test's `finally` will overwrite the live mutations of another concurrent
test. Consider using `t.before`/`t.after` per test or wrapping the mutation in a queue, and document
the assumed concurrency mode in a header comment.



─── tests/settings-inbox.test.mjs:291-297 ───
[test · medium] Filter-pipeline cache leaks across tests because `finally` invalidation is
conditional on `bumpInboxFilterGen` being exported. After `Object.assign(appState, saved)` restores
`appState.notifications` (etc.) to the previous fixture, the memoized array in
`getFilteredNotifications` still references the *test's* fixture, not the snapshot. If a sibling
test (or a later run in the same process) calls `getFilteredNotifications` before bumping, it will
receive cached rows built against the wrong fixture. The `if (typeof (inboxMod ||
m).bumpInboxFilterGen === 'function')` guard silently skips invalidation when the export is absent,
leaving the leak undetected. Always bump (or clear) the filter cache in `finally`, and require the
export as a precondition for these tests rather than skipping the invalidation.

─── tests/screen-invalidate.test.mjs:14-20 ───
[maintainability · medium] `captureRender` monkey-patches the global `process.stdout.write` without
any isolation guard. If `node:test` is run with `--test-concurrency` (or subtests are marked
parallel), concurrent renders will race on the same global stub and corrupt each other's captured
output. Even sequentially, if `render()` ever schedules async writes that invoke the callback, the
stub ignores the callback argument and may leave pending callbacks dangling. Recommend scoping the
capture to a per-call stream abstraction in `Screen.render(chunk => sink(chunk))` or at minimum
restoring `process.stdout.write` via a tracked flag and serializing the suite.

  function captureRender(s) {
    let out = '';
    const orig = process.stdout.write.bind(process.stdout);
-   process.stdout.write = (chunk) => { out += chunk; return true; };
-   try { s.render(); } finally { process.stdout.write = orig; }
+   const stub = (chunk, _enc, cb) => { out += chunk; if (typeof cb === 'function') cb(); return true; };
+   process.stdout.write = stub;
+   try { s.render(); } finally { if (process.stdout.write === stub) process.stdout.write = orig; }
    return out;
  }


─── tests/screen-invalidate.test.mjs:22-25 ───
[maintainability · medium] `positionMoves` only counts absolute CUP sequences (`\x1b[<row>;<col>H`).
It silently ignores relative cursor advances (`\x1b[<n>C`), SGR style resets, or any plain text
written without repositioning. A future optimization that switches to relative moves, or that emits
styles-only updates, would cause these tests to silently undercount and potentially pass for the
wrong reason. Either assert on actual emitted glyphs/content, or document the assumption in the
helper and add a comment that the tests are coupled to the absolute-position rendering strategy.

  function positionMoves(stream) {
+   // Counts ANSI CUP (Cursor Position) sequences; assumes Screen always
+   // uses absolute positioning. Update if Screen switches to relative moves.
    const m = stream.match(/\x1b\[\d+;\d+H/g);
    return m ? m.length : 0;
+ }
+ 
+ function emittedText(stream) {
+   // Strip ANSI escape sequences so tests can assert on rendered glyphs.
+   return stream.replace(/\x1b\[[0-9;?]*[ -/]*[@-~]/g, '');
  }

─── tui/config.mjs:87-98 ───
[security · high] Non-atomic plaintext write of a secret with TOCTOU on permissions.
`writeFileSync(TOKEN_FILE, token)` truncates and rewrites in place — an interrupted write (process
kill, ENOSPC, power loss) leaves a partial/corrupt PAT on disk. There is also a permission race: the
file is created with the process umask (often `0644` — world-readable) and only tightened to `0600`
in a separate `chmodSync` call afterward, so any process reading the directory in that microsecond
window sees the token. Use `writeFileSync` to a temp file in the same directory, `fsync` it, then
`renameSync` to `TOKEN_FILE`, and `chmodSync` the temp file *before* the rename so the final name is
never world-readable.

  // 2. Fall back to plaintext with strict permissions
    tokenStorageBackend = 'plaintext';
    if (!existsSync(CONFIG_DIR)) mkdirSync(CONFIG_DIR, { recursive: true });
-   writeFileSync(TOKEN_FILE, token);
-   // Lock down permissions so other users on a shared machine can't read the PAT.
-   // chmod is a no-op on Windows but harmless.
+   try { chmodSync(CONFIG_DIR, 0o700); } catch {}
+   const tmp = TOKEN_FILE + '.tmp';
    try {
-     chmodSync(CONFIG_DIR, 0o700);
-     chmodSync(TOKEN_FILE, 0o600);
-   } catch {
-     // Best-effort; ignore on platforms that don't support POSIX modes.
+     writeFileSync(tmp, token, { mode: 0o600 });
+     renameSync(tmp, TOKEN_FILE);
+   } catch (e) {
+     try { unlinkSync(tmp); } catch {}
+     throw e;
    }

─── tools/check-imports.mjs:155-156 ───
[bug · high] **paramRe under-binds and over-binds, causing silent false negatives.** Two failure
modes defeat the audit's purpose: (1) Only the FIRST identifier in a parameter list is captured —
`function f(a, b)` adds `a` but not `b`, so a parameter `b` that shadows an exported name (e.g.
`render`) will be wrongly flagged as "not imported". (2) The `=\s*` prefix alternation matches
arbitrary assignment chains such as `const foo = bar = baz`, marking `bar` as a phantom bound name —
so if `bar` were also an audit export, a real missing-import bug would be hidden. Also: doesn't
strip strings/comments before matching, so `paramRe` can match identifiers inside string literals
(e.g. `'function foo(x)'`) and incorrectly treat them as bound. Recommend reworking with a
paren-balanced parameter scanner that runs on the `clean` text.

- // Function parameters.
-   const paramRe = /(?:function\s*\w*|\(\s*|=\s*)\s*\(?([A-Za-z_$][\w$]*)\s*[,)=]/g;
+ // Function parameters — run on the comment/string-stripped text and
+   // walk a single parameter list with proper paren balancing so we
+   // capture every parameter (not just the first) and never match
+   // identifiers that live inside string/char literals.
+   function collectParams(clean) {
+     const out = new Set();
+     const fnDecl = /\b(?:async\s+)?function\s*[A-Za-z_$][\w$]*\s*\(/g;
+     const arrowAssign = /(?:const|let|var)\s+[A-Za-z_$][\w$]*\s*=\s*(?:async\s*)?\(/g;
+     const arrowBare   = /[=(,]\s*(?:async\s*)?\(/g; // arrow at start of arg list
+     for (const re of [fnDecl, arrowAssign, arrowBare]) {
+       let mm;
+       while ((mm = re.exec(clean)) !== null) {
+         let i = mm.index + mm[0].length; // points at char after `(`
+         // Walk params until matching `)`, balancing parens/braces/brackets
+         // and skipping strings/templates.
+         let depth = 1;
+         let buf = '';
+         while (i < clean.length && depth > 0) {
+           const c = clean[i];
+           if (c === "'" || c === '"' || c === '`') { i = skipString(clean, i); continue; }
+           if (c === '{' || c === '[' || c === '(') depth++;
+           else if (c === '}' || c === ']' || c === ')') { depth--; if (depth === 0) break; }
+           buf += c; i++;
+         }
+         for (const piece of buf.split(',')) {
+           const id = piece.trim().split(/[=:{}\[\]\s]/)[0];
+           if (/^[A-Za-z_$][\w$]*$/.test(id)) out.add(id);
+         }
+       }
+     }
+     return out;
+   }

─── tui/custom-keys.mjs:79-82 ───
[security · high] Shell-escape correctness on Windows: `%` is only special inside batch-file `%var%`
expansion (not for arguments passed via `cmd.exe /C` parsing through Node's `spawn({shell:true})`),
and `^!` only matters when delayed expansion is enabled. The blanket replacement can produce
commands that fail or change semantics, and `spawn(cmd, [], {shell:true})` on Windows wraps with
`cmd.exe /d /s /c "<cmd>"`, which interprets `%` differently again. Validate the escaping against
actual cmd.exe parsing or use `execFile`-style argument arrays instead of interpolating an escaped
string into a shell.



─── tui/custom-keys.mjs:198-211 ───
[bug · medium] Spawn lifecycle: `stdio: 'ignore'` discards stdout/stderr so users cannot diagnose
failures; the `timeout: 30000` option only sends SIGTERM and there is no `child.on('timeout', ...)`
listener or explicit `child.kill()`, so a child that ignores SIGTERM will leak; and calling
`render()` from the `exit` handler races with subsequent keypresses/UI updates. Consider piping
stdio through, attaching a timeout handler that kills the child, and only re-rendering when the
relevant UI state is still meaningful.

fail with cryptic shell errors. The placeholder table should be validated up front (warn that
required context is missing) or empty values should produce a clear user-facing error rather than a
silently-broken command.



─── tui/custom-keys.mjs:90-95 ───

─── tui/custom-keys.mjs:199-199 ───

─── tui/dialog.mjs:176-178 ───

─── tui/dialog.mjs:78-81 ───
[performance · low] `replaceDialog` calls `popDialog()` (which runs `restoreFocus` + `onClose` +

─── tui/focus.mjs:169-176 ───
[bug · high] When `zoneIndex` is `-1` (the initial dashboard state declared at the bottom of this
file), `prev = -2` and the modulo arithmetic `(-2 - i + zones.length) % zones.length` yields
`length-2` at `i=0` and ends up at `-1` (not `length-1`) on the final iteration because JavaScript's
`%` preserves the sign of the dividend. Two consequences: (1) the truly last zone (e.g.
`contributions`) is never visited on the first Shift+Tab from an unfocused dashboard, and (2) if
every prior zone's `canFocus()` returns false, the last iteration evaluates `zones[-1].canFocus()`
on `undefined` and throws a TypeError. Guard the initial `-1` state (e.g. start the search at
`length-1` when `zoneIndex < 0`) or normalize the modulo result before indexing.



─── tui/focus.mjs:228-228 ───
[bug · high] Every other branch in `getFocusedSelection` uses an `|| 0` fallback for

with no guard. When those fields are `undefined` (initial state, before the user has selected a
custom section/item), downstream consumers receive `{section: undefined, index: undefined}` and may
crash or render with `NaN`. Either fall back to `0`/empty string consistently, or have callers
handle the null case explicitly.



─── tui/focus.mjs:26-32 ───
[maintainability · medium] When the caller-supplied `token` does not match the current stack length,
the function returns silently with no log, warning, or counter. A misordered save/restore pair (e.g.
an exception thrown between `saveFocus()` and `restoreFocus()` so the inner frame is restored before
the outer) will leave the stack one entry deeper than the caller expects, and the symptom is only
that focus never returns — no diagnostic surface. At minimum emit a `console.warn` so nested-dialog
regressions surface during development.



─── tui/focus.mjs:126-131 ───
[bug · medium] `syncDashboardFocus` early-returns when the current tab is not the dashboard, but it
never *clears* `appState.dashboardCardsFocus` / `appState.dashboardFocusZone` in that case. If the

tab via `focusNext` / `focusPrev` — neither of which calls `syncDashboardFocus` — the dashboard
flags stay stale until the next explicit `resetFocus(0)`, and a render of the dashboard in the
interim will read inconsistent state. Clear the flags when `tab !== 0`, or centralize the cleanup in

─── tui/focus.mjs:92-98 ───
[maintainability · low] Every `canFocus` predicate reads `appState` fields that are populated
asynchronously by data loaders (`dashboardAttentionItems`, `events`, `dashboardRecentIssues`, etc.).

zero focusable zones and exit without updating `zoneIndex` or calling `render()`, leaving the user
with no visible focus indicator and no feedback that the keypress was handled. Consider a designated
always-focusable fallback zone per tab, or render an explicit empty/loading state when no zone is

multi-line comment above explicitly states the contract is "first row contains some leading
whitespace" (a loose claim), yet `assert.match(result[0], /^\s+$/)` requires the WHOLE first row to
be whitespace (`^...$` anchors, `\s+` covers the whole string). A reasonable wrap that produces "   
ind…" or "    indented" still preserves the indent (the contract from the comment) but fails this
assertion. Either weaken to /^\s+/ to match the stated contract, or update the docstring to clarify
the stronger guarantee.

-     const result = wrapText('    indented line that wraps', 8);
-     assert.match(result[0], /^\s+$/);   // first row is just indent
-     assert.ok(result.length > 1);        // actually wrapped (multiple rows)
+     assert.match(result[0], /^\s+/);    // first row preserves leading whitespace
+     assert.ok(result.length > 1);       // actually wrapped (multiple rows)

─── tui/keychain.mjs:117-128 ───
[security · high] The token is passed via `-w <token>` as a command‑line argument to `security`,
which means the PAT is visible in `ps aux` / `ps -ef` (and in any process accounting / audit logs)
for the lifetime of the process. On a multi‑user host this leaks the credential to any user who can
list processes — `secret-tool store` already demonstrates that this can be avoided by piping the
secret on stdin. Consider using stdin (`security ... -w -` with `input: token`) or a temporary file
written with 0600 permissions, and document the residual risk.



─── tui/keychain.mjs:179-186 ───

visible in `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process` / `wmic process` / process‑explorer style tools and in
any command‑line audit logging. This is similar to the macOS `-w` issue. If a stdin/secure‑string
alternative (e.g. `New-Object System.Management.Automation.PSCredential` populated via stdin and
written through `CredentialManager`/`CmdLet`) is feasible, prefer it; otherwise document the
exposure so consumers are aware that Windows native fallback is weaker than Linux/macOS native
paths.



─── tui/keychain.mjs:191-197 ───
[security · medium] The PowerShell command is built by JS string concatenation with overlapping
cmd.exe (outer `"..."`) and PowerShell (inner `'...'`/`\"`) quoting. Today `SERVICE`/`ACCOUNT` are
constants so this is safe, but any future change that makes them configurable, or a token derivation
that lands in `safeService`, would need a single, well‑tested escaping helper (or — better — a
base64‑encoded command line / script file) instead of three layered `replace` calls. As written the
layering is hard to audit and easy to break silently.

─── tui/github.mjs:278-283 ───
[bug · high] Registering SIGINT/SIGTERM handlers at module load time overrides Node's default "exit
on signal" behavior. The handlers only save caches and return without calling `process.exit()` (or
otherwise explicitly terminating). If anything else keeps the event loop alive (the TUI's raw TTY
input stream is the obvious candidate), Ctrl+C / `kill` will appear to do nothing — the user sees
caches saved but the process keeps running.

Suggested fix: have each handler either explicitly exit (`process.exit(0)`) after the synchronous
saves, or pass through to the default behavior (`process.removeAllListeners('SIGINT');
process.kill(process.pid, 'SIGINT');` style), so the TUI still gets a chance to run its own shutdown
but the process can never hang on the signal.

```js
process.on('SIGINT',  () => { saveEtagCache(); saveLastSynced(); process.exit(0); });
process.on('SIGTERM', () => { saveEtagCache(); saveLastSynced(); process.exit(0); });
```

  process.on('exit', () => {
    if (_cacheDirty) saveEtagCache();
    if (_syncedDirty) saveLastSynced();
  });
- process.on('SIGINT', () => { saveEtagCache(); saveLastSynced(); });
- process.on('SIGTERM', () => { saveEtagCache(); saveLastSynced(); });
+ let _shuttingDown = false;
+ function gracefulShutdown(code = 0) {
+   if (_shuttingDown) return;
+   _shuttingDown = true;
+   try { saveEtagCache(); } catch {}
+   try { saveLastSynced(); } catch {}
+   process.exit(code);
+ }
+ process.on('SIGINT',  () => gracefulShutdown(0));
+ process.on('SIGTERM', () => gracefulShutdown(0));


─── tui/github.mjs:751-756 ───
[bug · high] `downloadToFile` can delete a fully written, successfully downloaded file. The success
path is `out.on('finish') → out.close(cb) → resolve`. `cleanup()` sets `cleanupRequested = true` and
`out.on('close')` then unconditionally calls `removeDestination()`. But a late socket error on the
request *after* `finish` has already fired but *before* `out.close`'s callback runs will call
`cleanup()`, set the flag, and then when `out.close()` finally closes the stream the file is
unlinked — the caller resolves with `{ bytes, path }` for a path that no longer contains the data.
The race is narrow but real (TCP RST, server-side abort mid-close, etc.) and silently corrupts the
artifact.

Suggested fix: only mark the destination for removal when the request actually errored before the
body finished, e.g. track a `failed = true` set only inside the early-error paths, and have
`out.on('close')` delete only when `failed && cleanupRequested`.

+ let failed = false;
- function cleanup() {
+     function cleanup() {
+       if (settled) return;

─── tui/github.mjs:851-855 ───
[maintainability · low] `fetchTextUrl` leaks one `abort` listener on the shared `AbortSignal` for
every redirect hop. `abortHandler` is declared with `let` in the outer Promise scope and reassigned
by each recursive `get()` call; `cleanup()` removes only the *current* handler, so the prior
handlers stay attached to the signal forever (or until the controller is GC'd). On abort they all
run, each tries to destroy an already-completed request, and the catch swallows the throws — the
result is a minor memory/listener leak (up to 5 per fetch) plus a tiny amount of pointless work on
abort. Move `abortHandler` into `get()` and ensure `cleanup()` removes whichever handler is
currently registered before the recursive call.

Suggested fix: move `let abortHandler` inside `get`, register/remove it there, and call `cleanup()`
(or `signal.removeEventListener('abort', abortHandler)`) before recursing on a redirect so only one
listener is live at any time.

  req.on('error', e => finish(reject, e));
-       abortHandler = () => { try { req.destroy(); } catch {} finish(reject, new Error('Aborted')); };
-       signal?.addEventListener('abort', abortHandler, { once: true });
+       const onAbort = () => { try { req.destroy(); } catch {} finish(reject, new Error('Aborted')); };
+       signal?.addEventListener('abort', onAbort, { once: true });
+       req.on('close', () => signal?.removeEventListener('abort', onAbort));

─── tui/github.mjs:495-503 ───
[bug · medium] Second 304 after a forced refetch is reported to the caller as
`GitHubApiError('GitHub API error 304', 304, path)`, which surfaces in the UI as a hard API failure
even though the operation is conceptually benign (the server simply insists the cached body is still
valid). Two related issues:

1. The retry path deletes the stale cache entry *before* the retry, so the second 304 has no
fallback — even if the caller was happy with the previously cached body (which `If-None-Match`
implicitly confirmed is current), we throw it away and then error out.
2. The synthesized `304` error is misleading: callers/UI can't distinguish "real failure" from "we
already have fresh data but couldn't get the server to resend it". This was clearly a deliberate
loop-breaker, but it deserves to surface as a known recoverable case (either resolve with the last
known body if any, or reject with a distinct `NotModifiedError` carrying the 304 status).

Suggested fix: keep the last good body around (don't `etagCache.delete(cacheKey)` unconditionally —
only clear the etag so the next request skips `If-None-Match`), and on the second 304 resolve with
the cached body rather than rejecting. The one-retry cap is preserved; the user just gets their
data.

  // Cache was stale or empty. The 304 means the server has the
            // resource — our problem, not its problem. Re-issue the GET
            // without `If-None-Match` so the server sends a fresh body,
            // then re-cache it normally. Cap at one retry so a pathological
-           // server can't loop; second 304 falls through to normal error.
-           etagCache.delete(cacheKey);
+           // server can't loop. Keep the cached body around so a second
+           // 304 still resolves with known-good data instead of throwing.
            if (!o._retried304) {
-             return resolve(request(path, { ...o, _retried304: true }));
+             const prevBody = cached ? cached.body : null;
+             return resolve(request(path, { ...o, _retried304: true })
+               .catch(err => {
+                 if (err && err.status === 304 && prevBody !== null) return prevBody;
+                 throw err;
+               }));
            }


─── tui/github.mjs:186-199 ───
[maintainability · low] The on-disk ETag cache can grow without bound. In-memory LRU caps
`etagCache` at 500 entries, but `saveEtagCache()` serializes everything currently in the `Map` to
JSON, and `loadEtagCache()` only filters by TTL (`ETAG_TTL * 6` = 30 min). Combined with the 60 s
flush interval, on a long-running install every distinct `(api, method, path, accept, raw, token)`
combination ever observed is persisted until its 30-minute TTL elapses. The disk file is rewritten
whole each flush and can balloon to many MB before being trimmed on next load, and the TTL filter
only runs on *startup*, not at save time.

Suggested fix: cap the serialized snapshot in `saveEtagCache()` to `ETAG_CACHE_MAX` entries (sorted
by `lastAccess` desc) so the file mirrors the in-memory LRU; optionally also enforce a byte budget
so a single large raw response can't blow the file.

  function saveEtagCache() {
    if (!ETAG_CACHE_FILE) return;
    try {
-     const entries = [];
-     for (const [key, entry] of etagCache) {
-       entries.push({ key, etag: entry.etag, body: entry.body, ts: entry.ts, lastAccess: entry.lastAccess });
-     }
+     // Mirror the in-memory LRU cap on disk so the persisted file can't
+     // grow without bound between runs. Sort newest-access first so we
+     // keep the entries most likely to be reused.
+     const entries = [...etagCache.entries()]
+       .sort((a, b) => (b[1].lastAccess || 0) - (a[1].lastAccess || 0))
+       .slice(0, ETAG_CACHE_MAX)
+       .map(([key, entry]) => ({
+         key, etag: entry.etag, body: entry.body, ts: entry.ts, lastAccess: entry.lastAccess,
+       }));
      const dir = dirname(ETAG_CACHE_FILE);
      if (!existsSync(dir)) mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
      writeFileSync(ETAG_CACHE_FILE, JSON.stringify(entries));
      try { chmodSync(ETAG_CACHE_FILE, 0o600); } catch {}
      _cacheDirty = false;
    } catch { /* non-fatal */ }
  }

─── tui/palette.mjs:132-139 ───
[bug · medium] `execSelected` calls `close()` (which restores focus and clears
`paletteQuery`/`paletteCursor`) *before* invoking `a.run()`. Two consequences: (1) `a.run()` runs
synchronously against an already-closed palette, so any action that introspects palette state will
see inconsistent state; (2) the rejected-promise branch (`Promise.resolve(a.run()).catch(...)`) and
the synchronous `catch` will both surface *after* focus has been handed back to the previous widget,
so the resulting `showMessage` may target a different surface than the user expected. Capture the
action, defer `close()` until after the run completes (or at least until the first await), and
ensure errors are surfaced from the right focus context.

  export function execSelected() {

    const a = matches[appState.paletteCursor];
    if (!a) { close(); return; }
+   try {
+     const p = Promise.resolve().then(() => a.run());
+     close(); // tear down UI immediately so it doesn't flicker
+     p.catch(e => showMessage(e && e.message || String(e), 'error'));
+   } catch (e) {
+     showMessage(e && e.message || String(e), 'error');
-   close();
+     close();
-   try { Promise.resolve(a.run()).catch(e => showMessage(e.message, 'error')); }
-   catch (e) { showMessage(e.message, 'error'); }
+   }
  }

[bug · medium] importPortableConfig does JSON.parse(readFileSync(source, 'utf8')) with no try/catch

read+parse, stat the file first and reject anything above a sane limit (e.g. a few MB), and rethrow
a single user-friendly Error.

-   const bundle = JSON.parse(readFileSync(source, 'utf8'));

+     bundle = JSON.parse(readFileSync(source, 'utf8'));
+   } catch (err) {
+     if (err && err.code === 'ENOENT') throw new Error('Config bundle not found: ' + source);
+     if (err instanceof SyntaxError) throw new Error('Config bundle is not valid JSON: ' + err.message);
+     throw err;

+       else scrub(node[k]);
+     }
+   };

─── tui/recommended-features.mjs:49-52 ───
[bug · medium] The per-language comment regexes are unanchored and greedy. `/#.*/g`
(python/shell/yaml) matches the first '#' anywhere on the line, `/--.*/g` (sql) likewise, and the
default `//.*` matches the first '//' anywhere. So lines like Python `x = "# not a comment"`, Shell
`echo "issue #123"`, or `let s = "http://example.com"` will flag the rest of the line as a comment
and the inner quoted text is lost. Anchor the regexes (after optional leading whitespace) and/or
detect string spans first so their contents are excluded from comment detection. Note also that for
python/shell/yaml, `#`-style comments only apply inside actual code — yaml supports `#` only at
column 0 / after whitespace; the current regex will misclassify `#` inside heredocs or quoted
strings.

  const comment = language === 'python' || language === 'shell' || language === 'yaml'
-     ? /#.*/g
-     : language === 'sql' ? /--.*/g
-     : /\/\/.*|\/\*.*?\*\//g;
+     ? /^\s*#.*/g
+     : language === 'sql' ? /^\s*--.*/g
+     : /^\s*\/\/.*|^\/\*.*?\*\//g;

─── tui/security-aggregate.mjs:27-35 ───
[performance · medium] Repos are awaited serially in a `for` loop, each issuing three endpoint calls
in parallel. With `MAX_REPOS = 20` that's up to 60 sequential round trips with no retry/backoff, so
any rate-limit or transient failure surfaces as a `securityAggregateErrors` entry rather than a
transient retry. Consider a bounded concurrency pool across repos (e.g., 3–5 in flight) and respect
`Retry-After` / secondary rate limits.

─── tui/tabs/analyze-security.mjs:465-480 ───
[bug · high] Magic-number scroll math: `maxVisible = screen.height - 16` assumes a fixed chrome
height, but the actual rows rendered depend on the active sub-pane (`renderDependabotPane` uses
`maxH - 2`, secret/codescan use `maxH - 1`, advisories uses `maxH - 3`, deps uses `maxH - 2`). The
cursor/scroll position can therefore desync from the visible window, especially on small terminals
or when the header/error line pushes rows down. The maxVisible should be derived from the same
`maxH` passed into the active renderer's `rows` computation.

─── tui/tabs/analyze-security.mjs:73-76 ───
[security · medium] Raw API error messages are concatenated into user-facing banners without
sanitization. GitHub's error messages can leak details such as internal repo IDs, the exact missing
scope, rate-limit reset timing, or 404/403 distinctions that help an attacker enumerate access. Map
errors to coarse, user-safe strings and log the raw `e.message` only to an internal channel.

silently not reflected. Snapshot the sub-pane before awaiting and only refresh if it is still
`dependabot`, or call a dedicated dependabot reload.

[bug · medium] Pagination math treats paths+referrers as one contiguous scrollable list, which
produces surprising UX and a discontinuity at `start === paths.length`: while scrolling within paths
the referrers section is anchored to its top (refOff forced to 0), then jumps to a scrolled offset
the moment `start` crosses `paths.length`. Additionally, `start` is not validated against the total
`paths.length + referrers.length` before computing `refOff = start - paths.length`, so a stale
`detailsScroll` larger than both arrays yields a negative-ish/oversize offset (clamped only for
referrers, not validated for the outer `scrollIndicators`). Either (a) make each section
independently scrollable with its own offset, or (b) validate/clamp `start` at the top of the
function and document the single-list model.

-   const pathOff = Math.min(start, paths.length);
-   const refOff = start >= paths.length ? Math.min(start - paths.length, referrers.length) : 0;

+   const startClamped = Math.min(Math.max(start, 0), Math.max(totalRows - 1, 0));
+   const pathOff = Math.min(startClamped, paths.length);

─── tui/tabs/analyze-search.mjs:89-89 ───
[bug · high] Concurrent async handlers share the same `startAsync` scope names —
`submitSearch`/`submitUserSearch`/`submitCodeSearch`/`openUserRepos` use `'analyze-search-repos'`,
`'analyze-search-users'`, `'analyze-search-code'`, `'analyze-user-repos'`, which are also used by
`pageDown` and `loadMoreSearchResults`. `startAsync` aborts the previous controller for the scope
and bumps the generation, so a fast PageDown right after Enter silently invalidates the in-flight
submit: its `await` resolves, `isStale()` returns true, the page-1 results are discarded, and the
user is left with only page-2 rows (or no rows at all). Use distinct scope strings per operation
type, e.g. `'analyze-search-repos-submit'` vs `'analyze-search-repos-pagedown'`, so paging never
aborts a submit and vice-versa.

[bug · medium] `maxVisibleResults(contentH)` returns `contentH - 8`, assuming two footer rows (count
+ hint), but `renderRepoResults`, `renderCodeResults`, and `renderUserRepos` only paint one footer
row. On tight content heights this wastes a row; more importantly, the function is documented as the
"single source of truth shared by the renderer, keyboard nav, and mouse" — yet each renderer
silently compensates with its own assumptions, so the keyboard nav and the painted list can disagree
by one row when the list is short. Either pass a `hasHintRow` flag and let the helper compute the
right value, or compute it from the actual footer content the caller will paint.

+ export function maxVisibleResults(contentH, { hasHintRow = false } = {}) {
+   const footerRows = hasHintRow ? 2 : 1;
+   return Math.max(1, contentH - (6 + footerRows));
  }

segments. A caller passing `"../foo"` or `"a/../../etc"` results in `dir = '../foo'` (or similar),

combination with future code paths that might write through `rel`, it would be a path-traversal
sink. Reject any path containing a `..` segment up front.

─── tui/tabs/onboarding.mjs:110-110 ───
[bug · critical] Async onEnter race in the gh-CLI login branch: `() =>
import('./settings.mjs').then(m => m.loginWithGh())` returns a Promise. In `handleOnboardingKey`,
`const r = step.onEnter(); if (r !== false) stepIdx++;` treats any non-`false` return as success —
and a Promise is never `=== false` — so the wizard advances to step 3 before `loginWithGh()`
resolves. On login failure, the user is silently dropped on the "keyboard tips" step
unauthenticated. Either change the contract to `await` the returned promise (and only advance on a
non-false, settled value), or make async onEnter explicitly return `false` until the work completes.

─── tui/tabs/onboarding.mjs:263-266 ───

─── tui/tabs/inbox.mjs:207-218 ───
[bug · high] Partial-failure atomicity in batch mark-read loops. `markGroupRead` and `markAllRead`
mutate `m.unread` inline inside an `await` for-loop; if `markNotificationRead` throws mid-loop, the
`catch` block only shows an error toast — `bumpInboxFilterGen()` and `render()` are never invoked.
Already-mutated items then still appear as unread in the UI (filter cache still references the
pre-loop list and the screen is not redrawn), and any subsequent `markAllRead` against the same
group will retry already-marked ids. Either wrap in try/finally that always invalidates the cache +
re-renders, or stop on first failure and persist partial progress to the user.

— fine here, but the confirm dialog runs even when the user later navigates away. Consider capturing
a snapshot of `targets` and `token` inside the confirm callback to avoid acting on stale `appState`
if the user changes tabs/clears token mid-confirmation. Also: any `await` failure aborts the loop
but the count shown in the success path is never recomputed on failure, so the success-toast
template path is unreachable on error but the user gets no partial count.



─── tui/tabs/inbox.mjs:121-122 ───
[bug · high] Filter-cache key is fragile and can return stale results.
`Object.keys(appState.inboxSnoozed).length` does not capture expiry/timestamp changes — a snooze can
transition from active to expired (via the `now` comparison inside the filter) without changing the
key length, so the cached filter result silently keeps treating expired items as snoozed until
something else calls `bumpInboxFilterGen`. Additionally, `(appState.notifications._mutGen || 0)`
references a custom property on the array that is never assigned anywhere in this file —
`loadNotifications`/`loadMoreNotifications` replace the whole array, so this term only matters if
some other path mutates notifications in place without bumping, in which case it provides false
confidence. Replace the key with explicit signals (e.g., bump the gen on every mutation, or include
a hash of the snoozed map's active count + a `_mutGen` that you actually maintain).



─── tui/tabs/inbox.mjs:28-37 ───
[maintainability · medium] `loadSnoozedState` prunes expired snoozes from memory but never writes
the cleaned map back to `inbox-snoozed.json`. The on-disk file therefore accumulates dead ids across
runs and grows unbounded until the next manual `saveSnoozedState()` call (which only happens on user
snooze/unsnooze actions). After pruning, call `saveSnoozedState()` so disk state matches the
in-memory truth, and consider writing back lazily on every snooze/unsnooze mutation rather than
relying on read-time cleanup.



─── tui/tabs/inbox.mjs:261-269 ───
[bug · medium] `unsubscribeCurrent` sets `n.unread = false` after a successful unsubscribe, but on
GitHub the unsubscribe API does NOT mark a thread read — the thread remains unread on the server
until the user explicitly marks it (and will reappear on next sync). This local mutation diverges
state from the server: the 'unread'/'mentions' filters and the header unread count will silently
underreport, and `markAllRead` will skip it as already-read. Drop the `n.unread = false` line; if
the UX wants a visual cue, separate it from `unread` state (e.g., a local `_unsubscribed` flag) or
rely on the next refresh to reconcile.



─── tui/tabs/inbox.mjs:650-659 ───

─── tui/tabs/inbox.mjs:71-85 ───
[maintainability · medium] Cache invalidation on `loadMoreNotifications` is racy. The function
awaits the page, mutates `appState.notifications` and `appState.inboxPage`, then calls
`bumpInboxFilterGen()` and `normalizeInboxCursor()` — but only inside the success branch. If
`normalizeInboxCursor` is called while a previous fetch is in flight and a newer fetch has already
advanced `appState.inboxPage`, the cursor clamp uses the post-fetch length and selection may now
point past the appended items. More importantly, there is no debounce: if the user hammers Space,
multiple `loadMoreNotifications` calls overlap and each appends to `appState.notifications`, but
`appState.inboxPage` is read+written non-atomically — two concurrent calls both compute `page =
appState.inboxPage + 1` and append the same page twice. Guard with a single in-flight flag (or
capture the next page at call time and assert it matches `inboxPage + 1` before appending).



─── tui/tabs/inbox.mjs:381-382 ───

─── tui/virtual-scroll.mjs:129-132 ───
[bug · high] `getItemAtRow` JSDoc promises `-1` when no item is at the row, but the implementation

totalItems)`.

+   if (itemIndex < 0 || itemIndex >= totalItems) return -1;

  }

─── tui/virtual-scroll.mjs:28-30 ───

and the scroll bound is wrong. Pick one rounding policy and apply it consistently.

─── tui/toast.mjs:86-89 ───
[bug · high] Narrow-terminal layout break (correctness): `startX = W - 42` is negative whenever the
screen width is < 42 columns. The subsequent `boxW = Math.min(40, W - startX - 2)` then produces a
bogus width (>40 in many cases), and the `xx < W` guard in the styleBuf loop is the only thing
preventing an out-of-bounds write. The toasts will render at the wrong position with wrong width.
Clamp `startX` to `[0, Math.max(0, W - boxW)]` and recompute `boxW` accordingly.



─── tui/toast.mjs:99-102 ───

─── tui/toast.mjs:121-124 ───

─── tui/work-queue.mjs:7-7 ───
[bug · high] Sorting notifications by `n.last_read_at` is semantically wrong: a read
mention/review_request that the user just glanced at will float to the top even if the underlying
activity is old. `last_read_at` reflects user reading behavior, not item activity. Drop the fallback

─── tui/work-queue.mjs:3-3 ───
[maintainability · medium] Destructuring defaults (`notifications = []` etc.) only apply when the
property is `undefined`, not when the caller passes `null` or a non-iterable. `for...of` will then
throw `TypeError: object is not iterable` at runtime. Additionally, if the whole argument is `null`
the destructuring itself fails. Guard with `Array.isArray` (or coerce) before iterating.



─── tui/work-queue.mjs:18-18 ───
[bug · medium] Items without an `updated_at` all collapse to `''` and compare equal, so their
relative order is whatever `Array.prototype.sort` preserves (stable in V8 since 2018, but not
guaranteed by spec for legacy engines, and not stable across reloads if input ordering ever varies).
This produces UX flicker at the bottom of the queue. Add an explicit tie-breaker (e.g. compare by
`kind` then `id`) so date-less items have a deterministic order.



─── tui/work-queue.mjs:7-7 ───

reference to caller-owned objects. Any later mutation by the caller (or by a shared cache upstream)
silently changes the queue items. Either store only the fields you actually need or freeze a shallow
copy so the queue is genuinely a snapshot.



─── tui/which-key.mjs:11-17 ───

─── tui/undo.mjs:20-25 ───
[other · high] No protection against concurrent undo/redo calls. `undoStack` and `redoStack` are
module-level mutable arrays, and `undo()`/`redo()` are async. If the user double-presses the
keybinding (or two keybindings fire for the same action), two callbacks can interleave: each will
`pop` a different entry and race on `push`, leading to lost entries, out-of-order execution, or even
a partial state divergence with no error surfaced. Add an in-flight flag (or queue) at module scope
and bail out (or wait) when an operation is already running.



─── tui/undo.mjs:32-37 ───
[bug · high] Partial-failure recovery for undo/redo is unsafe and silently corrupts the stack. If
the callback partially mutates state (e.g. `addBookmark` succeeds) and then a later step throws, the
entry is pushed back onto the same stack, so the next undo/redo will retry the same broken operation
— but the stack now disagrees with actual app state (some side effects already applied, some not).
Either drop the entry on failure (so the user is told it can't be undone) and document the
limitation, or wrap callbacks so they are idempotent/compensating.



─── tui/undo.mjs:94-95 ───
[bug · medium] Manually mutating `repoData.stargazers_count` outside the store is fragile and will
drift. The same `repoData` object can be shared across views/list re-renders, and GitHub itself can
update the count concurrently (e.g. another client stars/unstars). Local +/-1 cannot recover from
any of those, so consecutive undo/redo cycles — or even a single one after a refresh — can display a
count that diverges from the real value. Prefer leaving the count alone and reconciling on the next
fetch, or persist the delta inside the store keyed by repo id.



─── tui/undo.mjs:8-8 ───
[maintainability · low] Dead/unused `data` field on undo entries. Every convenience wrapper builds a
`data` object, but `undo()`/`redo()` only invoke `entry.undo()` and `entry.redo()` and never read
`entry.data`. The captured closure variables are what actually drive the inverse operations. `data`
is therefore dead payload that can drift out of sync with the closures and mislead future
maintainers; either consume it for bookkeeping (e.g. status display) or drop it from the contract.



─── tui/undo.mjs:11-11 ───
[security · low] OAuth tokens are captured into long-lived closures stored in the undo/redo stacks
(up to `MAX_UNDO` = 20 entries, with no max on `redoStack` until a fresh action). This extends the
lifetime of credentials in memory far beyond the immediate API call, and amplifies the blast radius
if any future diagnostic dump, crash report, or serialization path ever exposes the stack contents.
Consider resolving tokens lazily at callback-execution time, or scrubbing the closures when an entry
is dropped or the stack is cleared.

─── tui/which-key.mjs:82-85 ───
[bug · high] `startSequence` does not guard `if (_active)` before calling `saveFocus()` and
assigning `_focusToken`. If it fires while the overlay is already open, the previously saved focus
token is silently overwritten and the prior focus context is lost forever (its `restoreFocus` will
never be called). Add an early-return / re-arm branch, or move the focus save/restore into a
`start/end` pair that is safe to call twice.



─── tui/which-key.mjs:87-91 ───
[bug · medium] The 2 s auto-close timer is never cleared on `close()`'s early-return path or on
overlapping startSequence calls in some races, and `setTimeout`'s callback calls `close()` which
itself `clearTimeout`s `_timeout`. If the timeout fires concurrently with another
`startSequence`/`close`, the order is non-deterministic. Additionally, pressing a valid prefix
repeatedly keeps extending the lifetime indefinitely (each prefix restarts the 2 s clock with no
upper bound). Consider tracking "is the overlay waiting for a second key" vs "timed out" and
clearing the timer explicitly in `handleKey` when a binding fires.



─── tui/which-key.mjs:24-30 ───

deliberately unimplemented (e.g., a `todo: true` flag) and either show a "not yet implemented"

─── tui/which-key.mjs:142-145 ───

─── tui/which-key.mjs:130-133 ───

staleness tag and have `loadAllReposBackground` resume from `appState.reposPage + 1` (skipping any

[bug · high] The OSC-52 path sets `success = true` purely on `process.stdout.write` not throwing.
The write returns a boolean (false on backpressure) and is in fact async — bytes may never reach the
terminal if stdout is piped to a slow consumer, redirected to `/dev/null`, or buffered. Worse, a
headless TTY stub will accept the bytes without forwarding them to any clipboard. The comment
justifies skipping `stdout.flush()` ("not part of Node's portable stream API") but that means this
branch will silently claim success in environments where OSC-52 cannot possibly work. Either fall
through to the tmux/native tools instead of short-circuiting, or check `process.stdout.isTTY` and
bail to fallback when it is false.

+   if (b64.length <= 75_000 && process.stdout.isTTY) {

─── tui/utils.mjs:5-14 ───
[bug · medium] Two missing-input hazards:
1. `new Date(iso).getTime()` returns `NaN` for any unparseable string, so `(Date.now() - NaN) / 1000
=== NaN` and the function returns `'NaNs'` (or `NaNm`, `NaNh`, …). Pane headers then render literal
`NaN` in the gutter.
2. Future dates (clock skew, scheduled-event times) yield negative `d`, which then produces strings
like `'-30s'` in the seconds branch.

Guard at the top: `const t = new Date(iso).getTime(); if (!Number.isFinite(t)) return '';` and
`const d = Math.max(0, (Date.now() - t) / 1000);`.

  export function relTime(iso) {
    if (!iso) return '';
-   const d = (Date.now() - new Date(iso).getTime()) / 1000;
+   const t = new Date(iso).getTime();
+   if (!Number.isFinite(t)) return ''; // unparseable input
+   const d = Math.max(0, (Date.now() - t) / 1000); // clamp future dates
    if (d < 60) return `${Math.floor(d)}s`;
    if (d < 3600) return `${Math.floor(d / 60)}m`;
    if (d < 86400) return `${Math.floor(d / 3600)}h`;
    if (d < 86400 * 30) return `${Math.floor(d / 86400)}d`;
    if (d < 86400 * 365) return `${Math.floor(d / 86400 / 30)}mo`;
    return `${Math.floor(d / 86400 / 365)}y`;
  }


─── tui/utils.mjs:166-171 ───
[bug · medium] The zero-width branch uses `cell >= lo && cell < hi`, but `cell` is never advanced
for combining/zero-width code points (see `displayWidth` which returns 0 for them). That means all
combining marks attached to the same base glyph share the base's post-cell position, and the
predicate selects/deselects them as a group based on the base's position rather than their own.
Concretely: for `é` (e + U+0301) at cells [5,6), the combining mark sits at cell=6 and is excluded
by `cell >= lo` whenever `lo > 6`, even though it's part of a glyph whose first cell IS selected.
Mouse text selection across CJK or emoji + VS16 sequences will drop trailing marks (and select
trailing marks that should not be). The correct check for a w=0 char is "the previous base char is
selected", e.g. track the last non-zero width end and use that, or recompute the base cell by
subtracting the accumulated zero-widths.

+   let lastBaseCell = 0;
    for (const ch of chars) {
      const w = displayWidth(ch);
-     const selected = w === 0 ? cell >= lo && cell < hi : cell < hi && cell + w > lo;
+     // For a w=0 char, decide based on whether its base glyph is selected.
+     const selected = w === 0
+       ? lastBaseCell >= lo && lastBaseCell < hi
+       : cell < hi && cell + w > lo;
      if (selected) out += ch;
+     if (w > 0) {
+       lastBaseCell = cell + w - 1; // the trailing cell of this base glyph
-     cell += w;
+       cell += w;
+     }
    }

[maintainability · low] Two related concerns in `runCommand` / `runCommandCapture`:
1. Both use `new Promise(async (resolve, reject) => { … })` — an async executor swallows synchronous
throws inside the executor (they become rejections of a resolved promise, which is fine) but loses
stack frames and confuses linters that flag unhandled-rejection paths. Prefer a plain executor with
`async` on the wrapping function.

imported at the top of the same file (line 374 imports `spawnSync`; `spawn` is the only dynamic
one). The dynamic import is per-call work that buys nothing. Use the top-level `spawn` import.

[maintainability · high] Ignoring `package-lock.json` is contrary to the npm/Yarn community
recommendation and most project templates (including npm-init, create-react-app, Next.js, etc.). The
lockfile should be committed so every developer and CI environment installs the exact same
dependency tree, which prevents "works on my machine" bugs and supply-chain drift. Remove this line
unless the project has a deliberate, documented reason (e.g., a library/template that intentionally
has no locked deps).

Let me draft the markdown now.


### Top Issues

1. **Credential leakage via process command lines.** `tui/keychain.mjs` passes the GitHub PAT as `-w <token>` to macOS `security` and as `cmdkey /pass:<token>` on Windows, exposing it in `ps aux`, `wmic`, and process-accounting logs. This must be migrated to a stdin/secure-string API where one exists, or at minimum to a pipe-based interface. Related: the `tui/utils.mjs` `openUrl` Windows branch passes user URLs through `cmd.exe`, which re-parses `&`, `|`, `^`, `%` etc. inside the URL itself.

2. **`tui/undo.mjs` has a syntax error that breaks the module entirely.** The redo callback's `Math.max(...)` is missing its closing `)` — the file fails to parse and every undo/redo path in the app is dead. Verify the matching `undo` callback and the initial action both close theirs. (This blocks the whole feature.)

3. **`tui/tabs/repos.mjs` cache clobber on starred repos.** `upsertEntity(r, { isOwner: false })` and `_seedStarredCache` both unconditionally pass `isOwner: false`, overwriting any cached `isOwner: true` for repos the user owns *and* stars. After starring one's own repo, owner-specific affordances silently disappear until the cache TTL expires. The codebase already has a `syncStarredEntities` helper that handles this correctly — use it.

4. **`tui/tabs/repos.mjs` stale-generation scope mismatch in `loadMoreRepos`.** The `[l]` handler creates `startAsync('repos-more')` but the background function checks `isStale(gen, 'repos')` — a *different* scope. The new generation never trips staleness, so a follow-up refresh silently does nothing, and the helper returns the wrong page. Also: `loadMoreRepos` / `loadStarredRepos` / `loadMoreStarred` skip the 401-recovery branch that `loadUserData` implements.

5. **Pre-mutation snapshot captured *after* mutation.** `tui/keys.mjs` star toggle captures `preStargazers` *after* `r.stargazers_count` has been updated, so the rollback restores the post-mutation value. Net effect: undo does nothing for stargazer changes, and a partial mutation leaves the repo count off by one forever.

6. **Non-atomic writes for secret-bearing and config files.** `tui/config.mjs` plaintext-token write uses `writeFileSync` directly; `tui/profiles.mjs` writes `profiles.json` directly; `tui/portability.mjs` `writeConfigFile` is direct; `tui/state.mjs` saves via the same pattern. A crash mid-write (ENOSPC, OOM kill, power loss) leaves a truncated file that the next parse-time `catch {}` then *erases on the next save* — silent data loss for users.

7. **Synchronous I/O in TUI render / event-loop hot paths.** `tui/plugins.mjs` `existsSync`+`readdirSync`+`readFileSync` per scan, `tui/store.mjs` `loadRepoPrefs` on every render, `tui/tabs/analyze-packages.mjs` `existsSync` inside an async function, `tui/tabs/repos.mjs` re-sort/filter/float pipeline per render. Any of these stalls keystroke-to-response latency and amplifies into DoS surface (e.g. hostile `plugin.json`).

8. **Module-level mutable singletons without isolation.** `tui/state.mjs` `appState` is mutated by every test and every tab handler with no synchronization. Comments throughout `tests/dashboard.test.mjs`, `tests/onboarding.test.mjs`, `tests/text-selection.test.mjs`, `tests/settings-inbox.test.mjs`, `tests/user-repos.test.mjs` all flag that tests pass only because of execution order, not because they're hermetic.

9. **Render-time state mutation anti-pattern.** `tui/tabs/analyze-compare.mjs`, `tui/tabs/analyze-security.mjs`, `tui/tabs/analyze-readme.mjs`, `tui/tabs/dashboard.mjs`, `tui/security-aggregate.mjs`, `tui/tabs/detail.mjs` all write to `appState.*` (scroll, bounds, sub-tab state) from inside their `render*` functions. This breaks idempotency, confuses callers, and racy with concurrent redraws.

10. **Hardcoded tab indices everywhere.** `tui/keys.mjs`, `tui/tabs/dashboard.mjs`, `tui/tabs/inbox.mjs`, `tui/tabs/repos.mjs`, `tui/tabs/settings.mjs`, `tui/tabs/analyze.mjs` all encode `tabState.current === 0/1/2/3/4/5` and `setTab(N)` with bare integers. Reordering `TABS` in `state.mjs` silently misroutes every navigation. Centralize via named constants from the tab registry.

### Module Hotspots

- **`tui/tabs/analyze-*.mjs`** — A cluster of eight tabs (`analyze-checks`, `analyze-compare`, `analyze-issues`, `analyze-packages`, `analyze-readme`, `analyze-search`, `analyze-security`, `analyze-traffic`) with a strikingly common set of defects: render-time mutation of `appState`, duplicate scroll/cursor helpers, hardcoded layout magic numbers, identical near-duplicate code paths between Issues and PRs / between Dependabot and Code Scanning, sequential `for/await` loops where `Promise.all` is correct, and stale-generation races. Density: ~6+ comments per file.

- **`tests/`** — Roughly 35 test files all exhibiting the same systemic issues: assertions on source-code substrings (`src.includes(...)`) instead of behavior, brittle exact-string UI assertions, shared `appState` without isolation, missing edge cases (empty inputs, malformed payloads, boundaries), `tryImport`/`spawnSync` error paths uncovered, deep-equality against implementation-defined values, monkey-patches of `process.stdout.write` and private fields, and snapshot lists that don't reflect the code's full read surface.

- **`tui/` core modules** — `keys.mjs`, `github.mjs`, `mouse.mjs`, `dialog.mjs`, `palette.mjs`, `screen.mjs`, `layout.mjs`, `input.mjs`, `state.mjs`, `theme.mjs`, `utils.mjs`, `virtual-scroll.mjs`, `work-queue.mjs`, `which-key.mjs`, `undo.mjs`, `custom-keys.mjs`, `git-context.mjs`, `keychain.mjs`, `bookmarks.mjs`, `plugins.mjs`, `profiles.mjs`, `portability.mjs`, `error-recovery.mjs`, `quick-settings.mjs`, `recommended-features.mjs`, `release-actions.mjs`, `store.mjs`, `repos-logic.mjs`, `toast.mjs`, `organizations.mjs`, `custom-sections.mjs`, `config.mjs`, `issue-create.mjs`, `select.mjs`, `render.mjs` — many carry 3–8 comments each and form the long tail of "dynamic import with no `.catch`, async race, unused import, dead code, magic number, render side-effect, strict-equality violation, security hole."

- **`tui/tabs/`** — Beyond the `analyze-*` cluster, `settings.mjs`, `repos.mjs`, `detail.mjs`, `dashboard.mjs`, `inbox.mjs`, `onboarding.mjs`, `help.mjs`, `files.mjs`, `forks.mjs`, `actions.mjs` carry the highest severity findings (cache clobber, missing 401 recovery, star rollback no-op, hand-rolled undo that overwrites watch modes, invalid `Date` crashes, regex literal typos like `/^\\d+$/` and `/\\r?\\n/` that match literal backslash+digit rather than CR/LF).

- **`tools/check-imports.mjs`** — The audit tool itself has bugs that defeat its purpose: only the first identifier in a parameter list is captured, the re-export branch duplicates the static-import parsing, and a dangling comment promises a "second pass" that doesn't exist. So the very tool that's supposed to catch unused imports is underreporting.

### Cross-Cutting Concerns

- **Fire-and-forget dynamic imports with no `.catch`.** Affects `tui/dialog.mjs` (all four `openXxx` helpers), `tui/custom-keys.mjs` (`runInternalAction`), `tui/mouse.mjs` (multiple click handlers), `tui/state.mjs` (`TABS.refresh`, `getToastModule`), `tui/github.mjs` (`fetchTextUrl`), `tui/render.mjs` (`import('./focus.mjs')`), `tui/screen.mjs` (`import('./render.mjs')`). A module that fails to load leaves the calling path silently unwired. Several of these also have async-import races (multiple `pushDialog`, multiple `execSelected`) where a double-tap duplicates the work.

- **Bare `catch {}` swallowing every failure mode.** Appears in `app.mjs` (401 recovery), `tui/config.mjs` (5 places), `tui/keychain.mjs`, `tui/profiles.mjs`, `tui/portability.mjs`, `tui/tabs/onboarding.mjs` (multiple), `tui/tabs/dashboard.mjs`, `tests/check-imports.test.mjs`, `tests/keychain.test.mjs`, `tests/bug-regressions.test.mjs`. None of them distinguish "expected empty" from "permission denied" / "disk full" / "module failed to parse". This is *the* dominant cross-cutting pattern.

- **Tests that don't test the contract they claim to.** Representative paths: `tests/recommended-features.test.mjs` (single-fixture dedup and sort-order tests that can't fail), `tests/contributions.test.mjs` ("caps at 3" test where all pages are inside the window), `tests/onboarding.test.mjs` (formatting-only assertions), `tests/settings-inbox.test.mjs` (`/0 issues/` regex far too loose, source-string greps in `mouse settings dispatch` and `session auto-refresh persistence`), `tests/state.test.mjs` ("keeps scopes independent" never bumps either scope), `tests/checks.test.mjs` (drops neutral conclusions from the assertion), `tests/utils.test.mjs` (relTime boundaries and `formatBytes(0)` never exercised). The pattern is "test exists, but a regression that broke the contract would still pass."

- **Render-time state mutation.** Cited in Top Issue 9, but worth re-stating as a pattern: `tui/tabs/analyze-compare.mjs`, `tui/tabs/analyze-security.mjs`, `tui/tabs/analyze-readme.mjs`, `tui/tabs/dashboard.mjs`, `tui/security-aggregate.mjs`, `tui/tabs/detail.mjs`. Clamping scroll, populating bounds maps, and writing `_focusZone` should live in key handlers / layout helpers, not in render.

- **Strict-equality violations (`==` / `!=`).** `tui/input.mjs`, `tui/repos-logic.mjs`, `tui/render.mjs`, `tui/recommended-features.mjs`, `tui/tabs/files.mjs` all carry the banned idiom. The repo also has `tools/check-imports.mjs` whose job is to catch unused imports; consider a parallel lint for `==`/`!=`.

- **Shared `appState` singleton mutated across tests without isolation.** `tests/dashboard.test.mjs`, `tests/onboarding.test.mjs`, `tests/text-selection.test.mjs`, `tests/settings-inbox.test.mjs`, `tests/user-repos.test.mjs`, `tests/bug-regressions.test.mjs`. Each file has individual `try { Object.assign(appState, saved) } catch {}` restorations but the snapshots are hand-maintained, often incomplete (missing fields the function under test actually reads), and the `afterEach` runs *after* assertions — a thrown assertion leaves state polluted for the next file.

- **Dead code / unused imports.** Wide-spread: `tui/issue-create.mjs` (`render`), `tui/tabs/dashboard.mjs` (`confirm`), `tui/tabs/analyze-security.mjs` (`sectionHeader`), `tui/tabs/repos.mjs` (`STALE_DAYS`), `tui/quick-settings.mjs` (`truncateToWidth`, `getUnreadCount`), `tui/toast.mjs` (`truncateToWidth`), `tui/which-key.mjs` (`truncateToWidth`), `tui/undo.mjs` (`appState`), `tui/tabs/onboarding.mjs` (`submitLogin`, `TOKEN_FILE`), `tests/input.test.mjs` (`createRequire`, `beforeEach`), `tests/keychain.test.mjs` (`mock`), `tests/theme.test.mjs` (`before`), `tui/custom-keys.mjs` (`getActionRegistry`), `tui/error-recovery.mjs` (`action`, `message` fields on `RECOVERY_PATTERNS`), `tui/tabs/files.mjs` (`resolve`), `tui/theme.mjs` (`tabActiveBg` byte-identical to `tabActive`), `tui/state.mjs` (duplicate `fileHistory`/`fileHistorySelected`, second block of dashboardContrib resets). The repo has the audit tool for this — it's underreporting.

- **Magic tab-index literals.** Already in Top Issue 10. Worth flagging as a pattern: 30+ call sites across `tui/keys.mjs`, `tui/tabs/dashboard.mjs`, `tui/tabs/inbox.mjs`, `tui/tabs/repos.mjs`, `tui/tabs/settings.mjs`, `tui/tabs/analyze.mjs`, `tui/tabs/onboarding.mjs`, `tui/which-key.mjs`.

- **Magic layout numbers without named constants.** Cited across `tui/tabs/analyze-checks.mjs` (5/30/37 column triple), `tui/tabs/analyze-readme.mjs` (paneTopY=9 duplicated), `tui/tabs/analyze-security.mjs` (maxVisible=screen.height-16 vs per-pane `maxH-N`), `tui/bookmarks.mjs` (`+6`, `H-4`, `boxH-5`), `tui/quick-settings.mjs` (`x + 6`, `x + boxW - valueText.length - 3`), `tui/palette.mjs` (W-4/H-4/boxW-8), `tui/which-key.mjs` (2000/40/+4/-2), `tui/tabs/help.mjs` (boxH-4 vs boxH-5 off-by-one).

- **Sequential async work that should be parallel.** `app.mjs` `actions` command (for-loop over up to 20 repos), `tui/custom-sections.mjs` (12 concurrent but no rate-limit handling), `tui/security-aggregate.mjs` (60 sequential round trips), `tui/tabs/analyze-packages.mjs` (release-asset fetches).

- **Non-atomic config/secret writes.** `tui/config.mjs`, `tui/profiles.mjs`, `tui/portability.mjs`, `tui/state.mjs` — all `writeFileSync` directly to the destination path with no temp-file + rename. Combined with silent `catch {}` parse failures, this is a silent-corruption class.

- **Auth-recovery gaps for expired tokens.** `tui/tabs/repos.mjs` `loadMoreRepos` / `loadStarredRepos` / `loadMoreStarred` skip the 401 reset branch that `loadUserData` implements. A user with an expired token gets stuck on a generic error message with no path to re-authenticate.

- **README / Markdown rendering does not sanitize control bytes.** `tui/tabs/analyze-readme.mjs` passes API-returned markdown straight to `wrapTextWithMap` / `screen.writeStr`. If the backend interprets CSI/OSC sequences, a hostile README can hijack the terminal.

- **Multiple regex literal typos that silently no-op.** `tui/tabs/actions.mjs` has `/^\\d+$/` (matches `\d` literal, not digits) and `/\\r?\\n/` (matches literal `\r` / `\n`); `tui/tabs/files.mjs` `/\\r?\\n/` is the same typo. These are real bugs, not style — workflow-log line splitting is broken today.

### Quick Wins

- **Fix the `tui/undo.mjs` syntax error first.** One missing `)` blocks the entire undo/redo feature. (Then audit for the same closure-balance in the corresponding `undo` callback and the initial action.)

- **Add a `.catch` to every fire-and-forget `import(...).then(...)`.** Sweep `tui/dialog.mjs`, `tui/mouse.mjs`, `tui/state.mjs`, `tui/render.mjs`, `tui/screen.mjs`, `tui/custom-keys.mjs`, `tui/github.mjs`. Most can be fixed with a 1-line `console.error` + `showMessage`; some need a real circuit-breaker.

- **Add a `package.json` `exports` map and `engines.node >= 20`.** Both flagged, both one-line.

- **Replace the literal-tab-index call sites with a named registry export from `tui/state.mjs`** (`TAB_INDEX.REPOS`, `TAB_INDEX.SETTINGS`, etc.). Mechanical search-and-replace, prevents an entire class of silent regressions.

- **Strip dead imports in one pass.** Run `tools/check-imports.mjs` (after fixing its under-binding bug — Top Issue / Hotspot note), then delete the 15+ unused-import lines identified across `tui/issue-create.mjs`, `tui/tabs/dashboard.mjs`, `tui/tabs/analyze-security.mjs`, `tui/tabs/repos.mjs`, `tui/quick-settings.mjs`, `tui/toast.mjs`, `tui/which-key.mjs`, `tui/undo.mjs`, `tui/tabs/onboarding.mjs`, `tests/input.test.mjs`, `tests/keychain.test.mjs`, `tests/theme.test.mjs`, `tui/tabs/files.mjs`.

- **Replace the `==` / `!=` sites** in `tui/input.mjs`, `tui/repos-logic.mjs`, `tui/render.mjs`, `tui/recommended-features.mjs`, `tui/tabs/files.mjs` with `===` / `!==` (or `??`). Banned by project rules; trivial mechanical fix.

- **Add a `pre`-write guard before mutating `r.stargazers_count` in `tui/keys.mjs`** so `preStargazers` is captured *before* the mutation. One-line reorder, fixes the silent rollback.

- **Use `syncStarredEntities` in `tui/tabs/repos.mjs`** instead of calling `upsertEntity(r, { isOwner: false })` directly. The helper exists and already handles the cached `isOwner: true` case.

- **Migrate all `writeFileSync(path, contents)` to a `writeConfigFile(path, contents)` helper** that does `write tmp` + `rename(tmp, path)` + `chmod 0o600`, and centralize it. Removes a whole class of "crashed mid-write, next save erased everything" bugs.

- **Replace the `/^\\d+$/` and `/\\r?\\n/` regex literals in `tui/tabs/actions.mjs` and `/\\r?\\n/` in `tui/tabs/files.mjs`** with the intended `^\d+$` and `\r?\n`. Real bugs, each a one-character edit.

- **Add the missing `.env*` entries to `.gitignore` and ship `package-lock.json`.** Two-line change, blocks the most common credential-leak failure mode in the repo.

- **Centralize tab-index constants, layout magic numbers, and the four `openXxx` dialog helpers** in `tui/dialog.mjs`. The four `openPalette` / `openHelp` / `openBookmarks` / `openQuickSettings` helpers are structurally identical; factoring them into one `openCenteredDialog(id, importPath, preState)` removes a quadruple of race-condition fixes in one go.

- **Replace the bare `catch {}` in `tui/config.mjs` token I/O and `tui/keychain.mjs` debug logging with `console.warn` at minimum**, and stop returning success from `setTheme` (`tui/theme.mjs`) when the persistence write failed.

- **Add the missing input-validation guards cited across the analyze-* tabs.** `repo.full_name.split('/')` without a length check appears in `tui/tabs/analyze-checks.mjs`, `analyze-compare.mjs`, `analyze-issues.mjs`, `analyze-readme.mjs`, plus the null-guard for `appState.repos` in `tui/issue-create.mjs`. A single helper `splitFullName(repo)` returning `{ owner, name } | null` would deduplicate the fix.
